## 2026-05-24 - Define Hardware-Independent ObjectNav Architecture

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-24-system-architecture.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-24-system-architecture.zh.html`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-24-system-architecture.md`

Change:
- Added the first formal system architecture design for Dual-Anchor Lifelong Semantic ObjectNav.
- Defined a three-layer development path: hardware-independent core, replay/simulation harness, and ROS 2 robot adapters.
- Captured core interfaces, future ROS 2 interfaces, data flow, failure modes, verification phases, and research relevance.
- Added a Chinese HTML reading version for easier review.
- Added a handoff note for the next implementation session.
- Refined Phase 1 into an indoor-first water-dispenser closed loop with outdoor-compatible anchor interfaces.
- Added fixed front-facing camera assumptions, verification viewpoint planning, stale/missing states, and relocation evidence behavior.
- Added Phase 1A fixture, forward-sector reveal, frontier viewpoint, discrete navigation, Pydantic model, SQLite/JSON memory, trial logging, metrics, and two-check missing confirmation decisions.
- Added a related-work positioning section covering OK-Robot, GOAT, 3D-Mem, DynaMem, OpenIN, SCOPE, R2F, SysNav, TrajRAG, and NavFoM.
- Added explicit research pain points: stale memory, missing-vs-localization ambiguity, model cost in high-frequency loops, verification viewpoints, dual-anchor uncertainty, and auditable trial logs.
- Added a staged model-integration policy that keeps Phase 1A deterministic and delays detector/VLM integration until offline replay evaluation.
- Updated the Chinese HTML reading version with the same related-work, pain-point, and model-integration guidance.

Reason:
- The project should develop and test the ObjectNav system away from the physical vehicle first, then integrate with the real FAST-LIO2 + PGO + Nav2 stack through stable adapters.
- This boundary improves reproducibility, baseline design, and paper defensibility.
- Recent ObjectNav work already uses semantic memory and foundation models, so the design must make the project's differentiation explicit before implementation starts.

Verification:
- Checked required documentation files exist with `test -f`.
- Inspected the design document headings with `rg -n "^## |^### " docs/design/2026-05-24-system-architecture.md`.
- Parsed the HTML reading page with Python `HTMLParser` and verified all internal navigation anchors resolve.
- Checked that the design Markdown and HTML reading page do not contain external links.
- Re-checked unresolved placeholders across the new docs and devlog with `rg`.
- Attempted a Playwright render check through the Node REPL, but `playwright` is not installed in the local Node environment; HTML verification used parser and anchor checks instead.
- Re-ran Markdown phase-heading checks after Phase 1A refinement.
- Re-parsed the HTML reading page with Python `HTMLParser` after adding the Phase 1A section and verified all internal navigation anchors resolve.
- Re-ran placeholder scans after Phase 1A refinement.
- Re-parsed the HTML reading page with Python `HTMLParser` after Phase 1A simulation/backend refinement and verified all internal navigation anchors resolve.
- Re-ran placeholder scans after Phase 1A simulation/backend refinement.
- Searched the design and handoff docs for the new fixture, reveal model, navigation backend, Pydantic, SQLite/JSON, trial logging, and yaw-scan terms.
- Checked arXiv page titles for the related-work links used in the design update.
- Re-parsed the HTML reading page after adding related-work/model-integration sections and verified all internal navigation anchors resolve.
- Re-ran placeholder scans after the related-work/model-integration update.

Effect:
- Future implementation can start from a documented architecture instead of coupling directly to robot-specific launch files, sensors, maps, or routes.
- The next concrete step is to write an implementation plan for the Phase 1A indoor water-dispenser closed loop.
- The paper positioning is clearer: the project should emphasize dynamic/lifelong memory repair, dual-anchor uncertainty, and hardware-independent reproducibility rather than claiming novelty from semantic memory alone.

Follow-up:
- Create the Phase 1A implementation plan for the deterministic indoor water-dispenser ObjectNav closed loop.
- Keep detector/VLM integration out of the main loop until deterministic Phase 1A and replay-based Phase 5 interfaces are verified.

Change:
- Installed `pytest` in the current Python environment after the user requested it.
- Added `.pytest_cache/` to `.gitignore`.
- Added `pytest` as a dev optional dependency in `pyproject.toml`.
- Added a Phase 1A implementation plan and switched it from `unittest` to `pytest`.
- Added a ROS-free `objectnav_core` Python package.
- Added ROS 2 `ament_python` package metadata for `objectnav_core` so the core can later be built by `colcon` on a ROS 2 machine.
- Added Pydantic models for anchors, poses, scene config, object observations, memory records, trial events, and trial metrics.
- Added the `straight_corridor_one_water_dispenser_unknown` grid fixture, forward-sector reveal, frontier extraction, frontier viewpoint planning, and wall-adjacent verification viewpoint planning.
- Added fake config-truth object observations and a deterministic discrete-step navigation client.
- Added SQLite memory storage with object records, observations, relations, trial events, indexes, and JSON export.
- Added a Phase 1A trial runner covering `discover_and_verify`, `reuse_same_start`, `reuse_different_start`, and `missing_and_relocation`.
- Added a CLI artifact runner exposed as `python -m objectnav_core.cli.run_phase1a --output ...`.
- Added future ROS 2 console script metadata for `objectnav_phase1a`.
- Added artifact outputs: `memory.sqlite`, `summary.json`, `memory_snapshot.json`, and `events.jsonl`.
- Added README quick-start instructions for running the Phase 1A tests.

Reason:
- The project needed a first executable vertical slice that proves the architecture independently from the real robot, detectors, VLMs, RTK, and Nav2.
- The implementation keeps the Phase 1A scope deterministic so later model and robot integration can be measured against a stable baseline.

Verification:

- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_simulation.py -v`; 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_trials.py -v` and observed the expected missing-module failure before implementing memory/trials.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_trials.py -v`; 2 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -v`; 12 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`; command exited successfully.
- Ran a ROS-coupling scan for `rclpy`, Nav2, TF, ROS messages, and costmap topic strings under `src/objectnav_core`; no matches were found.
- Ran a placeholder scan; matches were limited to historical devlog text, the plan's verification step, and the local SQL variable name `placeholders`.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -v`; 1 test passed, confirming `package.xml`, `setup.py`, and the ament resource marker exist without adding `rclpy` runtime imports.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -v` and observed the expected missing-module failure before implementing the CLI.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -v`; 1 test passed.
- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_phase1a --output runs/phase1a/latest`; command wrote the expected Phase 1A artifacts.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -v` after adding the CLI runner; 14 tests passed.

Effect:
- Phase 1A now has a runnable, tested, hardware-independent core slice.
- The core is packaged for future ROS 2 `ament_python` integration while remaining testable on this non-ROS computer.
- The first object memory reuse and missing/relocation state transitions are executable, though still deterministic and simplified.
- The deterministic trials can now be run outside pytest and saved as experiment artifacts.
- Future detector, replay, ROS 2, and Nav2 work can build against existing interfaces instead of replacing the core.

Follow-up:
- Add richer metrics persistence in `trial_metrics`.
- Add A* grid navigation before Nav2 adapter work.
- Add replay-image perception adapters only after the deterministic slice remains stable.

Reason:
- The Phase 1A handoff identified persisted `TrialMetrics` rows as the next required build step.
- Reports, baseline comparisons, and later experiment records need metrics available in SQLite, not only in transient model instances or `summary.json`.

Follow-up:

- Add baseline policy switches for nearest frontier and information gain after A* is stable.

## 2026-05-24 - Add A* Grid Navigation Backend

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/simulation/navigation.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_simulation.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-24-astar-grid-navigation.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-24-astar-grid-navigation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-24-phase1a-objectnav-core.md`

Change:
- Added a ROS-free `AStarGridNavigationClient` beside the existing straight-line discrete client.
- The A* client plans over 4-connected known free cells in `OccupancyGrid`; unknown and occupied cells are treated as blocked.
- The client keeps the existing navigation-client shape: `send_goal`, `cancel_goal`, `tick`, `status`, `path_length_m`, and `result_reason`.
- Added tests for obstacle detours, unknown-gap failure, and cancellation behavior.
- Added a design document and implementation plan for the A* backend.

Reason:
- The project needs a deterministic offline navigation backend for reachability and path-cost checks before ROS 2 Nav2 integration.
- A* supports reproducible baseline and ablation work while keeping the real robot execution boundary reserved for a future Nav2 adapter.

Verification:
- First ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_simulation.py -v` and observed the expected import failure for missing `AStarGridNavigationClient`.
- Re-ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_simulation.py -v`; 6 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -v`; 18 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`; command exited successfully.
- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_phase1a --output runs/phase1a/latest`; command wrote the Phase 1A artifacts.
- Queried `runs/phase1a/latest/memory.sqlite` directly and confirmed 4 successful `trial_metrics` rows remained present.
- Ran a core-only ROS-coupling scan; no matches were found under `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.

Effect:
- Offline navigation can now distinguish reachable, blocked, and unknown-space goals without ROS 2.
- The default Phase 1A trials still use the existing discrete client, so this change does not alter current artifact metrics.
- Future baseline policies can use A* path length instead of straight-line distance when scoring frontier and memory candidates.

Follow-up:
- Wire A* path-cost queries into baseline policy switches for nearest frontier and information gain.
- Add a ROS 2 Nav2 adapter design only after the offline backend and baseline policy behavior are stable.

Change:

- Added explicit `first_frontier`, `nearest_frontier`, and `information_gain` frontier policy behavior.
- Updated `Phase1ATrialRunner` to accept an optional `frontier_policy` while preserving the default `first_frontier` behavior.
- Added frontier-selection event payload fields for policy, candidate type, information gain, A* path cost, revisit penalty, and score.
- Added tests for nearest-frontier selection, information-gain selection, and the Phase 1A runner information-gain switch.
- Added a baseline policy design document and implementation plan.

Reason:
- The architecture and handoff both identified repeatable nearest-frontier and information-gain baselines as the next offline step.
- Baseline policies need explicit score terms before comparing memory-aware or semantic ObjectNav behavior.

- Confirmed the default `discover_and_verify` candidate types remain `frontier` and `object_verification`.
- Ran a core-only ROS-coupling scan; no matches were found under `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.

Effect:
- Future offline runs can explicitly select `nearest_frontier` or `information_gain` without changing ROS or Nav2 code.
- Baseline candidate scores now include enough structured terms for later reports and ablations.
- The default Phase 1A CLI artifact contract remains stable.

Follow-up:
- Add experiment report generation from `summary.json`, `events.jsonl`, and persisted `trial_metrics`, including policy score terms.
- Draft the ROS 2 Nav2 adapter design after offline report generation is stable.

Change:
- Added a design document for a future `objectnav_ros` package that will connect the ROS-free ObjectNav core to ROS 2, TF, OccupancyGrid costmaps, RViz markers, and Nav2 `NavigateToPose`.
- Defined the adapter/core boundary, default topics and actions, adapter parameters, data flow, failure modes, and verification plan.

Reason:
- Offline metrics persistence, A* path-cost checks, baseline frontier policies, and report generation are now stable enough to define the ROS integration boundary.
- The project needs an explicit Nav2 adapter design before creating ROS package code, so robot-specific assumptions do not leak into the research core.

Change:
- Restored the missing `objectnav_core.models` package with Pydantic models for anchors, poses, scene config, object observations, memory records, events, metrics, and the default corridor scene.
- Fixed `.gitignore` so the source package `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/models/` is no longer hidden by the root model-weight ignore rule.
- Added root-level ignores for ROS 2 `log/`, `build/`, and `install/` outputs.
- Updated the ROS 2/Nav2 adapter design to reflect the current ROS 2 Humble environment and the first buildable skeleton scope.
- Added a new `objectnav_ros` `ament_python` package.
- Added adapter modules for OccupancyGrid conversion, PoseStamped/TransformStamped conversion, Nav2 goal/status conversion, and JSON `std_msgs/String` object-observation conversion with stale timestamp rejection.
- Added a minimal `rclpy` node shell with configurable goal, observation, status, selected-goal, costmap, Nav2 action, frame, anchor, memory, and stale-observation parameters.
- Added launch/config files and adapter unit tests.
- Regenerated the latest Phase 1A summary and report after restoring the core scene model.

Reason:
- The environment now has ROS 2 Humble, so the next handoff item was to create a buildable adapter skeleton instead of leaving ROS integration design-only.
- The core model package had been hidden by an over-broad `.gitignore` pattern, which made the repository unable to reproduce its own tests from tracked files.
- The adapter must translate ROS types at the boundary while keeping ObjectNav state and memory logic ROS-free.

## 2026-05-24 - Add Mock-Testable Nav2 Action Client

File:
- `README.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-24-ros2-nav2-adapter.md`

- `src/objectnav_ros/objectnav_ros/nodes/objectnav_node.py`

Change:
- Added `Nav2NavigationClient`, a thin asynchronous wrapper around Nav2 `NavigateToPose`.
- The wrapper waits for the action server, sends goals, records feedback messages, handles goal acceptance/rejection, maps action results to core `NavigationStatus`, preserves Nav2 error details on failures, and supports cancel requests.
- Updated `objectnav_adapter` node initialization so it owns a configured Nav2 client without automatically sending navigation goals.
- Added fake-action-client tests for unavailable server, successful result, rejected goal, aborted result detail, and cancel request behavior.
- Updated README, design, and handoff notes to reflect that a mock-testable action client exists, while live Nav2 and robot tests remain future work.

Reason:
- The previous adapter only had message conversion and status mapping. The next integration step needed an explicit Nav2 action boundary that can be tested without a live robot or Nav2 server.
- Keeping the wrapper in `objectnav_ros` preserves the ROS-free core boundary.

Verification:
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core:src/objectnav_ros:${PYTHONPATH} python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_ros/tests/test_nav2_status_mapping.py -q`; 7 tests passed.

- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core:src/objectnav_ros:${PYTHONPATH} python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_ros/tests -q`; 17 tests passed.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core:src/objectnav_ros:${PYTHONPATH} python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests src/objectnav_ros/tests -q`; 38 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_ros/objectnav_ros`; command exited successfully.
- Ran a core-only ROS-coupling scan; no matches were found under `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran `colcon build --packages-select objectnav_core objectnav_ros --event-handlers console_direct+`; both packages built successfully.
- Ran an installed-space `Nav2NavigationClient` smoke test with a fake unavailable server; it returned `FAILED` and `action_server_unavailable`.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 colcon test --packages-select objectnav_core objectnav_ros --event-handlers console_direct+`; 21 core tests and 17 adapter tests passed.
- Ran `colcon test-result --verbose`; summary reported 38 tests, 0 errors, 0 failures, 0 skipped.

Effect:
- The ROS adapter now has a test-covered action-client boundary ready for mocked action-server and replay tests.
- The node can be launched with a configured Nav2 client, but it still does not drive the robot or execute the ObjectNav state machine.

Follow-up:

- The synthetic replay follow-up is handled by the later "Add ROS 2 Synthetic Replay And RViz Markers" entry.

Change:

- Changed synthetic observations so they are published only after the replay robot has detector range/FOV/line-of-sight visibility.
- Added `/objectnav/debug_markers` with a small RViz text legend showing replay step and base pose.

Reason:
- The first RViz screenshot was technically correct but visually ambiguous: it looked like a static map with a goal and did not explain which parts were replay scaffolding.
- Before moving to Gazebo or robot tests, the replay should make the adapter boundary easier to inspect and should exit cleanly during repeated launch-smoke runs.

## 2026-05-25 - Decide TurtleBot3 Gazebo Assumed-Target Path

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-25-turtlebot3-gazebo-assumed-target.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-25-turtlebot3-gazebo-assumed-target.md`

Change:
- Reviewed the current architecture, ROS 2/Nav2 adapter design, synthetic replay design, and handoff notes before choosing the next integration direction.
- Added a design document for a TurtleBot3 Gazebo + Nav2 smoke slice that treats `water_dispenser` as an assumed configured map-frame target pose instead of requiring detection.
- Added a handoff documenting local TurtleBot3/Nav2/Gazebo package availability, required environment variables, map files, commands already run, risks, and next implementation steps.

Reason:
- The current synthetic replay proves adapter topic and RViz visibility, but it does not exercise a live Nav2 action server.
- A TurtleBot3 open-source simulation is a better next Gazebo step than a vehicle-specific stack because it is reproducible, hardware-independent, and already installed locally.
- Deferring perception is deliberate: using an assumed target pose isolates the Nav2 execution boundary before detection noise can hide integration bugs.

Verification:
- Ran `git status --short --branch`.
- Read `README.md`, `docs/design/2026-05-24-ros2-nav2-adapter.md`, `docs/design/2026-05-25-ros2-synthetic-replay-rviz.md`, `docs/handoff/2026-05-25-ros2-synthetic-replay-rviz.md`, `docs/design/2026-05-24-system-architecture.md`, and related handoff/design docs.
- Ran `source install/setup.zsh && ros2 pkg list | rg -i "turtlebot|nav2|gazebo|slam_toolbox|map_server|amcl"`; TurtleBot3, Nav2, Gazebo ROS, map server, AMCL, and slam_toolbox packages were present.
- Ran `which gazebo`, `which gz`, `which ign`, and `which ros2`; all expected commands were present.
- Listed installed TurtleBot3 Gazebo and Navigation2 launch, world, map, parameter, and RViz files under `/opt/ros/humble/share`.
- Ran `source install/setup.zsh && TURTLEBOT3_MODEL=waffle ros2 launch turtlebot3_navigation2 navigation2.launch.py --show-args`; launch arguments printed successfully.
- Observed that the same Navigation2 launch fails with `KeyError: 'TURTLEBOT3_MODEL'` when that environment variable is unset.
- Inspected `/opt/ros/humble/share/turtlebot3_navigation2/map/map.yaml`; the map has nonzero origin `[-10.0, -10.0, 0.0]`.
- Inspected `src/objectnav_ros/objectnav_ros/adapters/costmap_adapter.py` and confirmed current core grid conversion does not preserve `OccupancyGrid.info.origin`.

Effect:
- The next Gazebo step is now explicit: use TurtleBot3 + open-source map + configured assumed `water_dispenser` pose to test live Nav2 action execution.
- The design avoids prematurely mixing perception, map-origin frontier selection, and full ObjectNav state-machine integration into the first simulator run.

Follow-up:
- Implement `assumed_target_nav2_smoke` and a TurtleBot3 launch wrapper.
- Choose and verify one reachable target pose in the TurtleBot3 map.
- Add origin-aware map/costmap conversion before using full frontier exploration on TurtleBot3 maps.

## 2026-05-25 - Implement TurtleBot3 Assumed-Target Nav2 Smoke

Change:
- Added `assumed_target_nav2_smoke`, a thin ROS node that maps a configured semantic class (`water_dispenser`) to a map-frame `Pose2D`, publishes `/objectnav/selected_goal`, waits for `/objectnav/goal`, and sends the pose through `Nav2NavigationClient`.
- Added `turtlebot3_assumed_targets.yaml` with a first assumed `water_dispenser` target at `(x=1.5, y=0.0, yaw=0.0)` in `map`.
- Added `turtlebot3_assumed_target_nav2.launch.py`, which sets `TURTLEBOT3_MODEL=waffle` and can start TurtleBot3 Gazebo, TurtleBot3 Navigation2, and the smoke node.
- Kept `send_goal_on_start=false` by default so AMCL can be initialized before triggering the semantic goal.
- Added tests for target validation, trigger matching, status JSON, node trigger behavior, config presence, launch references, and entry-point installation.
- Updated README, design, and handoff instructions for the TurtleBot3 assumed-target smoke.

Reason:
- The synthetic replay path verifies adapter topics and RViz outputs, but it does not exercise a live Nav2 action server.
- TurtleBot3 simulation is the lowest-risk open-source bridge to test the Nav2 action boundary before Gazebo worlds with custom semantics or real robot trials.
- Treating `water_dispenser` as a configured pose deliberately avoids mixing perception, projection, and navigation execution in the first simulator smoke.

Verification:
- Ran focused assumed-target tests; 5 tests passed.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core:src/objectnav_ros:${PYTHONPATH} python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests src/objectnav_ros/tests -q`; 58 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_ros/objectnav_ros`; command exited successfully.
- Ran `python3 -m py_compile src/objectnav_ros/launch/objectnav_adapter.launch.py src/objectnav_ros/launch/synthetic_replay.launch.py src/objectnav_ros/launch/synthetic_replay_rviz.launch.py src/objectnav_ros/launch/turtlebot3_assumed_target_nav2.launch.py`; command exited successfully.
- Ran a core-only ROS import scan; no matches were found under `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran `colcon build --packages-select objectnav_core objectnav_ros --event-handlers console_direct+`; both packages built.
- Ran installed-space checks; ROS 2 found `assumed_target_nav2_smoke`, and `turtlebot3_assumed_target_nav2.launch.py --show-args` exposed the expected launch arguments.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 colcon test --packages-select objectnav_core objectnav_ros --event-handlers console_direct+`; 21 core tests and 37 adapter tests passed.
- Ran `colcon test-result --verbose`; summary reported 58 tests, 0 errors, 0 failures, 0 skipped.
- Ran isolated node-only launch smoke with `start_gazebo:=false start_nav2:=false use_sim_time:=false`; `/objectnav/status` and `/objectnav/selected_goal` were published from installed space.
- Ran isolated trigger smoke without a Nav2 server; publishing `water_dispenser` to `/objectnav/goal` produced an `action_server_unavailable` status as expected.

Effect:
- The project now has a concrete, reproducible entry point for the first live Nav2 action-server test using open-source TurtleBot3 assets.
- The first Gazebo smoke can proceed without requiring object detection or a custom simulated water-dispenser model.

Follow-up:
- Run the full TurtleBot3 Gazebo/Nav2 launch, set the initial pose in RViz, trigger `/objectnav/goal`, and record the Nav2 result.
- If the assumed pose is blocked or unreachable, adjust only `turtlebot3_assumed_targets.yaml`.
- Add origin-aware occupancy-grid conversion before using core frontier exploration on TurtleBot3 maps.

Change:

Reason:

- The smoke needs to be reproducible offline before it is useful as the bridge from synthetic replay to real robot work.

Verification:
- Ran a direct Gazebo factory probe and reproduced the missing `/spawn_entity` condition while Gazebo tried `http://models.gazebosim.org`.
- Ran a Gazebo probe with `GAZEBO_MODEL_DATABASE_URI=""`; `/spawn_entity`, `/delete_entity`, and `/get_model_list` appeared after 2 seconds.
- Ran `python3 -m py_compile src/objectnav_ros/launch/turtlebot3_assumed_target_nav2.launch.py`; command exited successfully.

- Ran `bash -lc 'source /opt/ros/humble/setup.bash; colcon build --packages-select objectnav_ros --symlink-install'`; package built successfully.
- Ran the full TurtleBot3 Gazebo/Nav2 smoke in `ROS_DOMAIN_ID=78`; `/spawn_entity` was ready after 2 seconds, `/odom` and `/navigate_to_pose` were available, the initial pose was set to `x=-2.0`, `y=-0.5`, `yaw=0.0`, and triggering `water_dispenser` produced `/objectnav/status` with `navigation_status=SUCCEEDED` and `result_reason=nav2_succeeded`.
- Ran `bash -lc 'source /opt/ros/humble/setup.bash; PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 colcon test --packages-select objectnav_core objectnav_ros --event-handlers console_direct+'`; 21 core tests and 37 ROS tests passed.
- Ran `colcon test-result --verbose`; summary reported 58 tests, 0 errors, 0 failures, 0 skipped.

Effect:
- The project now has a verified open-source simulator bridge from assumed semantic target to live Nav2 action execution.
- The `water_dispenser` assumed target at `(x=1.5, y=0.0, yaw=0.0)` is verified reachable in the TurtleBot3 map when initialized at `(-2.0, -0.5, 0.0)`.
- Gazebo Classic startup no longer depends on the online model database for this wrapper launch.

Follow-up:
- Add origin-aware `OccupancyGrid` handling before feeding TurtleBot3 maps into full core frontier exploration.
- Consider turning the successful smoke procedure into a documented manual script or launch test.
- Keep detector/perception integration deferred until the Nav2 and map-frame contracts remain stable.

## 2026-05-26 - Redesign Algorithm Around Usability-Centered Object Memory

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.zh.html`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.md`

Change:
- Added a new design document that reframes the algorithmic contribution from dual-anchor alignment to usability-centered lifelong object memory.
- Added a Chinese HTML reading version explaining each algorithm step and the main derivations.
- Defined a memory belief split into `P_existence`, `P_location_valid`, and `P_usable`.
- Defined delayed birth, delayed promotion, evidence classification, event-driven usability update, finite-budget trust/verify/search/retire decision-making, path-cost cache invalidation, and opportunistic verification.
- Explicitly demoted dual-anchor RTK/LIO alignment to infrastructure rather than the main paper contribution.
- Added a handoff note for the next implementation/design iteration.
- Updated the design with the real-vehicle boundary: `dual-anchor-lifelong-objectnav` owns algorithm/replay, while `XJTLU-autonomous-vehicle` owns RTK, RGB-D, Nav2, FAST-LIO2/PGO, rosbag collection, and live-robot closure.
- Added concrete XJTLU launch modes, rosbag topics, future RGB-D/detector interfaces, and an intermediate `TraceEvent` contract.
- Updated the HTML reading version with an XJTLU interface section and MathJax display-math support for rendered formulas.

Reason:
- The dual-anchor architecture was strong as a systems direction but too close to engineering integration as an algorithmic paper contribution.
- Real RGB-D and SLAM conditions make perfect FREE-space negative evidence rare, so the new design focuses on memory usability and retirement rather than proving object disappearance.
- The method needs a clear path for single-researcher progress: deterministic simulation, real trace collection, trace-driven replay, then small live robot closure.

Verification:
- Created the Markdown and HTML files.
- Re-checked the updated Markdown for the new XJTLU interface section.
- Re-checked by text search that the updated docs contain the XJTLU interface section, relevant ROS topics, `TraceEvent`, and MathJax configuration.
- Scanned the new design, HTML, and handoff files for unresolved template placeholders; no matches were found.
- An earlier lightweight HTML parser/anchor check passed before the later XJTLU/MathJax update; it was not repeated after the user requested not to check HTML.
- Did not run browser rendering because the user asked not to check HTML rendering.
- Did not run runtime tests because this task only changed documentation.

Effect:
- The next implementation target is now the smallest testable usability-memory slice: add `P_usable`, retired/archive state, non-confirmation evidence, and trust/verify/search/retire decision metrics to the ROS-free core.
- Future detector, RGB-D, RTK, and ROS integration can be evaluated against the usability-memory algorithm instead of becoming the paper's core novelty.

Follow-up:
- Implement a deterministic Phase 1A extension for usability belief and memory retirement.
- Add a trace schema for RGB-D/detector/pose/costmap evidence collection.
- Use real logs to measure FREE/OCCLUDED/UNKNOWN/NON_CONFIRMATION ratios before any full live-robot ObjectNav closure.

Change:
- Added a ROS-free usability-memory model with `P_existence`, `P_location_valid`, `P_usable`, evidence events, and a bounded trust/verify/search/retire decision policy.
- Added deterministic tests for ghost-memory retirement, false-deletion protection, positive recovery, quarantined negative evidence, and expected-cost decisions.
- Added a stress runner that writes `summary.json`, `decision_boundary.csv`, and `stress_report.html` under an output directory.
- Registered the runner as the `objectnav_usability_stress` console script and documented the command in the README.

Reason:
- RTK and RGB-D integration are not ready yet, but the core mathematical assumptions can already be attacked offline.
- The project needs a small executable harness before adding real detector/depth/SLAM traces, otherwise the algorithm discussion stays too abstract to falsify.
- The first slice deliberately avoids ROS, Nav2, RTK, GroundingDINO, and real robot dependencies.

Verification:

- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`; command exited successfully.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`; 30 tests passed.
- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_usability_stress --output runs/usability_stress/latest --seed 13 --monte-carlo-runs 200`; command exited successfully and wrote `summary.json`, `decision_boundary.csv`, and `stress_report.html`.
- The fixed-seed stress run produced decision counts: trust `58`, verify `10`, search `102`, retire `30`.
- The stress run confirmed the three intended synthetic scenario outcomes: ghost retired, false-deletion guard not retired after positive recovery, and quarantined OOD negative evidence unchanged.

Effect:
- There is now a concrete pre-robot algorithm test target for the new usability-centered route.
- Future detector, depth, pose, and path-cost interfaces can be validated by converting their outputs into evidence events and replaying them through this harness.
- The generated stress artifacts live under ignored `runs/usability_stress/latest/` and can be regenerated from the recorded command.

Follow-up:
- Add adversarial stress cases for nearby same-class objects and wrong data association.
- Add stale cost-cache and path blockage cases before relying on `D_verify` in real-robot decisions.
- Convert future XJTLU RGB-D/RTK/Nav2 logs into trace events and compare real evidence ratios against these synthetic assumptions.

## 2026-05-26 - Fix Budget-Clipped Trust Cost Pathology

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.zh.html`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-26-usability-memory-stress-harness.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-26-usability-centered-lifelong-object-memory.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/memory/usability.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_usability_memory.py`

Change:
- Added a regression test for a fixed-seed sample where `P_v=0.0324`, `D_verify=2.3922`, and `C_search=57.5906`.
- Removed per-branch `min(B_remaining, ...)` clipping from the trust/search/verify expected-cost calculation.
- Updated the design docs to state that `B_remaining` is a feasibility constraint and diagnostic, not an expected-cost cap.
- Updated the experiment report with the corrected fixed-seed decision counts.

Reason:
- Budget clipping made likely failure look artificially cheap. In the failing sample, trust won because both trust and verify were clipped near `B_remaining`, leaving verify to look worse only because it paid the extra verification cost.
- This creates the bad behavior the design was meant to avoid: when budget is low, the robot gambles on a nearly invalid memory instead of acknowledging the failure branch cost.

Verification:
- Ran the new regression test before the fix; it failed with `DecisionType.TRUST`.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_usability_memory.py -q`; 9 tests passed after the fix.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`; 31 tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`; command exited successfully.
- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_usability_stress --output runs/usability_stress/latest --seed 13 --monte-carlo-runs 200`; command exited successfully.
- The corrected fixed-seed stress run produced decision counts: trust `6`, verify `19`, search `136`, retire `39`.
- The problematic sample now has `cost_trust=76.22`, `cost_verify=58.52`, `cost_search=57.59`, and no longer chooses trust.

Effect:
- Low remaining budget no longer converts a high-probability failed trust action into an artificially cheap gamble.
- The current pure expected-cost policy may still prefer search over verify when `P_v` is extremely low. Choosing opportunistic verification before search requires an explicit memory-maintenance or information-value term, not hidden budget clipping.

Follow-up:
- Add a separate design/test for opportunistic verification value if the system should verify cheap old memories before global search.
- Add budget feasibility reporting so actions whose raw expected cost exceeds `B_remaining` are visible without distorting their costs.

Change:
- Added a design document for the lightweight 2D grid trace generator layer.
- Added `generate_grid_trace`, which emits fixed-seed sequential trace events with robot pose, target pose, evidence type, evidence strength, path-blocked state, association-candidate count, and navigation costs.
- Added `run_grid_trace_experiment`, which replays trace events through `UsabilityUpdater` and `UsabilityDecisionPolicy`.
- Added a CLI entry point, `objectnav_grid_trace_experiment`.
- Added tests for deterministic generation, required trace schema, artifact writing, and packaging.
- Documented the command in the README and recorded the first fixed-seed experiment report.

Reason:
- The isolated usability stress harness is useful but does not produce sequential traces.
- A fast 2D generator lets the project attack logic failures before Habitat noisy-depth replay and XJTLU real-bag replay are ready.
- The trace schema gives future Habitat/rosbag converters a concrete target.

Verification:
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grid_trace_experiment.py -q`; 2 tests passed.

- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_grid_trace_experiment --output runs/grid_trace/latest --seed 17 --episodes 12 --steps-per-episode 8`; command exited successfully and wrote `summary.json`, `events.csv`, and `trace_report.html`.
- The fixed-seed run produced 96 events and decision counts: trust `20`, verify `61`, search `2`, retire `13`.
- The fixed-seed run covered evidence types: positive `20`, non-confirmation `16`, unknown `25`, occluded `14`, access-blocked `11`, free `8`, scene-changed `2`.
- The OOD scenario produced 16 quarantined events and left belief unchanged.
- The removed/moved and blocked-access scenarios retired with final `P_usable` below `0.1`.

Effect:
- The project now has a reproducible pre-Habitat trace layer that exercises sequential belief and decision behavior.
- Future Habitat noisy-depth and XJTLU bag replay should target the same intermediate event columns instead of integrating directly into the belief update.

Follow-up:
- Add stale path-cost and inflated-obstacle blockage scenarios.
- Add true multi-object association replay beyond the current `association_candidates` field.
- Replace hand-set event probabilities with Habitat and real-bag statistics as soon as those traces exist.

Change:
- Added `inflated_corridor_block` and `stale_path_cost` scenarios to the 2D trace generator.
- Added path-cost fields for obstacle/body intersection, inflation-layer intersection, stale-cost state, cached path costs, fresh path costs, and costmap revision.
- Added stale-vs-refreshed decision logging with `decision_stale`, `decision_refreshed`, `decision_flipped_after_refresh`, and `stale_cache_error`.
- Changed the grid trace experiment runner to stream event rows directly to CSV while accumulating summaries, so 100000 episodes can run without storing all rows in memory.
- Added summary metrics for decision rates, inflation-blocked events, stale-cost events, decision flips after refresh, stale cache error rate, and mean cached-to-fresh cost ratio.
- Updated the README and experiment report to use the 100000-episode Monte Carlo command.

Reason:
- The first 12-episode trace had a verify-heavy bias because verification cost was usually much lower than search cost.
- Inflation-layer blockage and stale path-cost cache are the most direct way to stress the budget/path-cost decision logic before Habitat or real robot data exists.

Verification:
- Ran the updated grid trace tests before the implementation; they failed because the new scenarios and path-cost metrics were missing.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grid_trace_experiment.py -q`; 2 tests passed after implementation.

- Ran `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_grid_trace_experiment --output runs/grid_trace/latest --seed 17 --episodes 100000 --steps-per-episode 8`; command exited successfully.
- The 100000-episode run produced 800000 events with decision counts: trust `139377`, verify `365502`, search `147338`, retire `147783`.
- The run produced path-cost metrics: inflation-blocked events `175000`, stale-cost events `100000`, decision flips after refresh `149923`, stale cache error rate `0.875`, and mean cached-to-fresh cost ratio `15.7098`.
- Checked artifact size: `events.csv` has 800001 lines including the header and is about 242 MB.

Effect:
- Verify remains the largest decision class, but the decision distribution is no longer collapsed around verify. Search and retire each reached about 18%.
- The 2D layer now directly tests the stale path-cost failure mode that can make a real robot over-verify or trust a bad route.
- The generated large Monte Carlo artifacts stay under ignored `runs/grid_trace/latest/`.

Follow-up:
- Add true multi-object association replay beyond `association_candidates`.
- Sweep inflation radius, cache refresh thresholds, and opportunistic verification value.
- Replace synthetic path-cost transitions with Habitat and real XJTLU costmap-derived transitions when available.

- Path-cost metrics remained active: inflation-blocked events `155554`, stale-cost events `88888`, decision flips after refresh `133269`, stale cache error rate `0.875`, mean cached-to-fresh cost ratio `15.7189`.

Follow-up:
- Replace the hand-coded JPDA-lite margin gate with trace-calibrated association likelihoods.

Change:
- Added a Habitat-Sim direct RGB-D-semantic stress runner.
- Added synthetic YOLO-breaker corruptions for semantic target masks: `miss`, `fly_point`, `edge_break`, and `mixed`, plus `clean` baseline.
- Added mask-quality metrics and evidence conversion for corrupted semantic masks.
- Added CLI entry point `objectnav_habitat_semantic_yolo_stress`.
- Added tests for optional Habitat imports, miss/fly-point breaker behavior, fragmented mask rejection, missed visible target handling, out-of-view target handling, structural-category filtering, and component stats.
- Ran a 5-episode smoke and a 30-episode semantic stress run on HM3D `GLAQ4DNUx5U`.
- Added an experiment report and updated Habitat design/handoff docs.

Reason:
- The previous Habitat replay used synthetic target distance as evidence. The user asked to use Habitat semantic sensor output and explicitly attack the usability-memory system with detector-like semantic-mask failures.
- Semantic masks provide a stronger intermediate layer before adding a real YOLO/GroundingDINO detector.

Verification:
- Verified Habitat-Sim direct semantic sensor observations on HM3D `GLAQ4DNUx5U`; the semantic mask is a `uint32` id image and the scene reports 908 semantic objects / 124 categories.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_semantic_yolo_stress.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`; 7 tests passed after first implementation.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_semantic_yolo_stress.py -q`; 8 tests passed after the out-of-view absence fix.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`; command exited successfully.
- Ran a 5-episode semantic smoke; it produced all five breaker modes and showed miss collapse plus one mixed false-positive-positive row.
- Ran a 30-episode semantic stress after fixing out-of-view target absence to `UNKNOWN`; it completed with 210 rows.
- Final 30-episode metrics: evidence counts `positive=71`, `non_confirmation=13`, `unknown=126`; decision counts `verify=176`, `trust=34`; mean final `p_valid=0.819062`.
- Breaker mode means: clean `0.908939`, miss `0.610724`, fly-point `0.847893`, edge-break `0.866955`, mixed `0.860801`.
- Stress failures observed: mixed produced 9 false-positive-positive rows; miss produced 16 missed-visible-target rows.

Effect:
- The project now has a semantic-mask stress layer that is materially closer to detector failure than distance-proxy replay.
- The current memory policy handles many fragmented/edge-corrupt masks conservatively as `UNKNOWN`, but can still be inflated by single-frame false positives that survive quality gates.

Follow-up:
- Add temporal consistency and multi-view confirmation before accepting positive semantic/detector evidence.
- Add debug mask PNG export for representative corrupted frames.
- Replace corrupted oracle semantic masks with real detector replay.

Effect:
- The current semantic memory runner no longer accepts isolated one-frame positive masks by default.
- The official start pass shows that scripted actions see the target in only 6 of 30 episodes and 27 of 210 rows, so full ObjectNav evaluation needs a navigation policy rather than static scripted sweeps.
- The stricter confirmation gate reduces false positives but also lowers final memory confidence when real target views are sparse.

Follow-up:
- Add debug PNG export for suppressed false-positive candidates and missed visible targets.
- Tune confirmation thresholds adaptively by category, mask area, range, and motion.
- Replace corrupted oracle masks with real detector replay after debug export is available.

## 2026-05-27 — Decision: publication target is robotics systems venues, no benchmark grinding

Files changed:
- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md` (new)
- `docs/decisions/2026-05-27-publication-target-robotics-systems-no-benchmark-grinding.md` (new)

Change summary:
- Reviewed the four 2026-05-26..27 Habitat experiment reports and audited
  `objectnav_core/cli` + `objectnav_core/evaluation` runners. Confirmed
  that all current Habitat work corrupts the oracle semantic mask (not the
  RGB), runs a fixed 6-step action sequence, never revisits the target,
  never persists memory across episodes, and does not exercise
  `objectnav_core/planning/`.
- Wrote a design doc proposing an RGB-noise + real open-vocab detector +
  revisit controller + lifelong memory harness pipeline as the sim-to-real
  validation path, with a 6-cell experiment matrix
  (`clean/mild/heavy` × memory `on/off`) and custom memory metrics.
- Recorded a decision to target **ICRA / IROS / RA-L / CoRL** and to
  explicitly **not** push for HM3D ObjectNav leaderboard numbers, because
  the single-episode SPL/success protocol does not measure cross-episode
  memory reuse, which is the central contribution. The design doc above is
  the planned validation strategy under this decision.

Reason:
- The 0.909 → 0.757 → 0.683 → 0.626 drop across the four reports is not an
  algorithm regression; it is the test setup exposing harder categories,
  stricter confirmation, and (under `episode_start`) target invisibility.
  Continuing to push on the current mask-corruption stress does not move
  the algorithm toward the user's real goal (deployment on a real robot
  after sim validation).
- Vision-venue benchmark grinding would require integrating a navigation
  policy and re-running full HM3D val with reimplemented baselines, at
  2–3× the engineering cost, while still hiding the lifelong contribution
  inside single-episode SPL.

Verification:
- None executed in this entry. The decision and design are paper artifacts
  only. Verification will be performed when the design is implemented:
  unit tests for the noise pipeline, a Habitat smoke run, and the
  6-cell experiment matrix described in the design doc's Verification Plan.

Effect on future work:
- Next implementation work: `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/simulation/rgb_noise.py`,
  a detector adapter, a revisit controller, and a lifelong memory harness,
  wired into a new CLI `run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress`.
- The existing mask-corruption runners stay as unit-test scaffolding for
  `UsabilityUpdater`, but are no longer the headline experiments.
- The Open Questions section of the design doc lists 6 items requiring
  user input before implementation starts (detector choice, noise-profile
  provenance, revisit strategy, depth noise in v1, memory persistence
  backend, oracle-stop acceptability).

  target decision.

  separate decision record per item, and lets implementation start
  directly against concrete formulas and configs.

Change:
- Added explicit YOLO-World prompt modes: `target`, `all_categories`, and `target_aliases`.
- Changed the RGB-noise ObjectNav harness default from full-category YOLO prompting to target-conditioned prompting.
- Raised the default Habitat RGB sensor size for this harness from 96 px to 320 px.
- Added trace metadata for the detector prompt categories used in each row.

Reason:
- The first YOLO-World smoke missed the visible `toilet` target, blocking real-detector validation.
- Debugging showed that 64/96 px frames were too small, while 320 px frames detected early target views but the full 6-category prompt set let `bed` outcompete `toilet` on several visible frames.
- ObjectNav knows the current goal category, so target-conditioned detector prompting better matches the task interface.

Verification:
- Added failing tests for the new defaults and prompt-mode validation.

## 2026-05-27 - Add ObjectNav Stop Semantics To RGB-Noise Harness

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`

Change:
- Added `stop_on_trust`, defaulting to `true`, so an episode ends once the policy chooses `TRUST` while the target is visible.
- Added `--stop-on-trust` / `--no-stop-on-trust` CLI support.
- Added per-row oracle target view metrics: `oracle_bbox`, `oracle_bbox_area`, `oracle_bbox_fill_ratio`, `oracle_touches_edge`, `oracle_touches_side_edge`, `oracle_edge_sides`, and `oracle_edge_clearance_ratio`.

Reason:
- The target-conditioned YOLO smoke reached `TRUST` at step 5, then the runner kept walking through edge-clipped target fragments and decayed the belief. That continuation is a harness artifact, not ObjectNav STOP behavior.
- The new view metrics make later edge-clipped misses visible in the trace instead of mixing them with true detector failures.

Verification:
- Added failing tests for the new default, stop helper, and edge-clipped view metrics.
- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py -q`; 7 tests passed after implementation.

- Ran `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress --output /tmp/rgb_noise_preflight_stop --noise-levels clean --detector yolo_world --memory-ablation on --max-episodes 1 --preflight-only`; summary reported `stop_on_trust=true`.
- Ran `git diff --check`; no whitespace errors.
- Extended the edge metric test to distinguish floor/bottom contact from left/right side clipping, then reran the focused and combined test commands; 7 and 19 tests passed respectively.

Effect:
- The harness now reports ObjectNav-style success without artificially continuing past the first valid stop.
- Full-trace debugging remains available with `--no-stop-on-trust`.

  `grounding_dino_max_image_side=384`.

  visibility.

Reason:

  category qualification to see whether all six target categories become ready.

Change:
- Ran the first memory-only / replay-style Habitat matrix on `badger-linux`
  with fixed `out_and_back` trajectories, Grounding-DINO, RGB/depth noise, and
  memory `on/off`.
- Used `1280x720` Habitat RGB/depth observations and detector-side
  `grounding_dino_max_image_side=384`.
- Restricted the matrix to detector-qualified or partially usable categories:
  `bed`, `sofa`, `toilet`, `plant`, and `tv_monitor`.
- Excluded `chair` because the previous qualification showed first-N chair
  samples were blocked by oracle target visibility.
- Aggregated memory/noise/category/revisit-proxy metrics and wrote a Markdown
  experiment report plus a Chinese HTML status report.

Reason:
- The user asked how to validate the memory system without a navigation system,
  then approved a memory-only replay simulation with fixed trajectory,
  Grounding-DINO, RGB/depth noise, and memory `on/off`.

Verification:
- Remote preflight reported `detector=grounding_dino`,
  `sensor_resolution=1280x720`, `noise_levels=clean,mild,heavy`,
  `memory_ablation=on,off`, and `stop_on_trust=false`.

  `runs/habitat_usability/memory_only_grounding_dino_replay_1280x720_epc2_cap384`.
- Remote artifact check confirmed `summary.json` and `rgb_noise_trace.csv`
  exist and summary fields report `60 900 268 1280x720 grounding_dino 384`.
- Local aggregation from the trace found memory `on` produced 372 trust rows
  and 268 oracle-stop success rows, while memory `off` produced zero trust rows
  and zero success rows.

Effect:
- The replay harness now provides evidence that the memory update and decision
  path turns repeated detector evidence into trust on fixed trajectories.
- The strongest current categories are `bed`, `sofa`, and `toilet`.
- `plant` and `tv_monitor` expose false-trust / sparse-visibility risk and
  need stronger current-view or geometric stop gating.

Follow-up:
- Add an `episode_local` ablation to separate within-episode accumulation from
  cross-episode lifelong memory.
- Add a current-view confirmation gate for stop/trust decisions.
- Implement visibility-aware episode selection before reintroducing `chair`.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Naive-Count Baseline And Shared Trust Gate

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added `memory_ablation=naive_count` as a non-memory accumulating baseline.
- Kept `naive_count` deliberately simple: it only counts positive evidence
  inside the current replay; non-confirmation and unknown observations do not
  affect it, and the first positive observation immediately creates the naive
  belief instead of using delayed birth.
- Added a shared decision-side current-positive gate for all memory modes:
  raw `TRUST` is allowed to remain `TRUST` only when the current frame has both
  oracle target visibility and positive detector evidence.
- Wrote `raw_decision`, gated `decision`, `decision_gate_reason`, and
  `naive_positive_count` to the trace so false-trust can be measured directly.
- Added `raw_decision_counts` and `decision_gate_reason_counts` to run
  summaries.

Reason:
- The previous `off` baseline was a strawman single-frame sanity check and did
  not accumulate evidence.
- `plant` and `tv_monitor` false-trust came from allowing historical memory
  confidence to act as a stop decision without current-frame confirmation.
- The user clarified that the baseline must not borrow non-confirmation,
  unknown handling, or delayed birth from the proposed algorithm, and that the
  trust gate must be shared by all three modes rather than only changing
  metrics or only benefiting memory `on`.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for accepting `naive_count`, positive-only naive
  accumulation, shared current-positive decision gating, and summary reporting
  of raw/gated decision counts.
- Ran
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py -q`;
  16 tests passed after implementation.
- Ran
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_rgb_noise.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_depth_noise.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_yolo_world_adapter.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grounding_dino_adapter.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_revisit_controller.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_lifelong_memory_harness.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_semantic_yolo_stress.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_usability_memory.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`;
  50 tests passed.
- Ran preflight with `--memory-ablation on,naive_count,off`; summary included
  `naive_count`, `sensor_resolution=1280x720`, and `stop_on_trust=false`.

Effect:
- The next replay experiment can compare memory `on` against a fairer
  accumulating-but-naive baseline.
- False-trust is now visible as raw `TRUST` rejected by the shared decision
  gate, instead of being hidden behind post-hoc metric changes.

Follow-up:
- Pull on `badger-linux` and run focused tests in `conda habitat`.
- Run a small Grounding-DINO replay smoke with `on,naive_count,off`.
- Re-run the full memory-only replay matrix and update the experiment report.

  count but does not cross the trust threshold.

  its role as an accumulating comparison.

  `NAIVE_COUNT_TRUST_P_VALID`, non-confirmation and unknown do not change the
  count, and two positives cross the trust threshold.

Effect:
- `naive_count` is now a minimal accumulating positive-count baseline rather
  than a single-frame strawman.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Shared Detector Area Filter

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added a shared detector-side maximum area filter before detections enter the
  evidence classifier.
- The filter rejects individual over-broad boxes and also rejects the whole
  detector mask if the union of remaining boxes is still over-broad.
- Defaulted `max_detection_area_ratio` to `0.70`; CLI values `<=0` disable the
  filter for ablations.
- Added `detection_filtered_count` to trace rows and total filtered detection
  count to summaries.

Reason:
- The gate-rejection PNGs showed `tv_monitor` detections that covered most or
  all of the image while Habitat GT had zero target pixels. Those full-frame
  detector boxes should not become positive memory evidence.

Verification:
- Added failing tests for filtering over-broad detector boxes, dropping
  over-broad union masks, and preserving old behavior when the filter is
  disabled.
- Ran the focused RGB-noise stress tests; 22 tests passed.
- Ran the broader focused suite; 56 tests passed.
- Ran CLI preflight and confirmed `max_detection_area_ratio=0.7` is recorded.

Effect:
- The next Linux diagnostic replay can test whether rejecting pathological
  full-frame boxes materially reduces `tv_monitor` false positives without
  changing memory or gate logic.

Follow-up result:
- Linux reruns showed the conservative `0.70` filter reduced out-of-view
  positive rows from `158` to `128` while preserving all `36` visible positive
  rows and all `21` oracle-stop success rows.
- An aggressive `0.40` sweep reduced out-of-view positives further to `102`,
  but also removed all plant visible positives and cut oracle-stop success rows
  to `11`.
- Keep `0.70` as the default; stricter area thresholds are not a safe detector
  fix for `plant`.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Structured Naive-Count Decision Challenge

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/grid_trace_experiment.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grid_trace_experiment.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-26-2d-grid-trace-generator.md`

- `docs/README.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Extended the 2D grid trace replay to emit both `usability_memory` and
  `naive_count` policy decisions on the same events.
- Kept `naive_count` positive-only with a two-positive threshold; it does not
  consume non-confirmation, unknown, scene-change, delayed birth, JPDA, or
  geometry.
- Applied the same current-positive decision gate to both policies and wrote
  raw/gated decision columns to `events.csv`.
- Changed `removed_or_moved` to start with two positive observations before
  non-confirmation / scene-change evidence, so the baseline reaches trust
  honestly before stale evidence arrives.
- Added baseline comparison summaries and an HTML section for the generated
  trace report.
- Ran a local 900-episode structured challenge and documented the result.

Reason:
- The Habitat fixed replay was too friendly to positive counting. A structured
  room/corridor-style decision challenge is needed to show where memory,
  negative evidence, and association guards matter beyond `naive_count`.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for the changed `removed_or_moved` sequence and baseline
  comparison output, then implemented until they passed.
- Ran
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grid_trace_experiment.py -q`;
  3 tests passed.
- Ran the experiment command:
  `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m objectnav_core.cli.run_grid_trace_experiment --output runs/grid_trace/structured_naive_count_decision_challenge_seed17_ep900 --seed 17 --episodes 900 --steps-per-episode 8`.

Effect:
- `naive_count` produced `1241` unsafe raw-trust rows versus `882` for
  `usability_memory`.
- In `removed_or_moved`, `naive_count` produced `600` unsafe raw-trust rows
  after early positives, while `usability_memory` produced `7` and retired.
- In `multi_object_association`, `naive_count` showed `200` false-positive
  write-pressure rows while the JPDA-style memory path recorded zero.
- The next Habitat task should port this structure into a physical replay
  rather than continuing only with simple fixed-room trajectories.

Follow-up:
- Build or select Habitat routes with room/corridor revisits, target
  disappearance/reobservation, blocked access, stale cost refresh, and nearby
  same-class distractors.
- Keep the shared gate and positive-only `naive_count` semantics unchanged.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Geodesic Path Long-Range Replay Protocol

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added `replay_protocol=geodesic_path`.
- Added CLI/config parameter `--geodesic-path-max-steps`.
- The protocol queries Habitat-Sim's navmesh shortest path from the configured
  start to the goal viewpoint, downsamples waypoints, teleports through
  `approach` frames, and finishes with repeated goal-viewpoint `confirm`
  frames.
- Episode summaries now include approximate replay `path_translation_m` plus
  official episode geodesic/euclidean metadata when available.
- The replay loop initializes motion accounting from the first replay step
  when that step is a teleport, so path summaries do not count temporary
  measurement poses used while constructing the replay.

Reason:
- The previous visibility challenge was a useful memory/evidence stress test,
  but it was still a local teleport protocol. The project needs a long-range
  bridge from official ObjectNav starts before moving to a full action-level
  follower or robot run.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for the new preflight parameter, path resampling, and
  geodesic replay step generation.
- Confirmed the new tests failed before implementation:
  `3 failed, 32 passed`.
- Implemented the protocol and reran focused local tests:
  `39` tests passed.
- Ran CLI preflight with `--replay-protocol geodesic_path` and
  `--geodesic-path-max-steps 12`; summary recorded `approach` phase and the
  max-step value.
- Ran compile and whitespace checks.

Effect:
- The next Linux run can replay official episode-start to goal-viewpoint
  paths while keeping detector, RGB/depth noise, memory modes, `naive_count`,
  and the shared gate unchanged.
- This is still not official Habitat ObjectNav success/SPL because waypoints
  are teleported rather than reached by an action-level policy.

Follow-up:
- Pull the commit on `badger-linux`.
- Run an `oracle_bbox` geodesic-path smoke first, then a small Grounding-DINO
  geodesic-path replay from `episode_start`.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Current-Positive Opportunistic Trust

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/memory/usability.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_semantic_yolo_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added `DecisionContext.current_positive_evidence`.
- Added a shared `UsabilityDecisionPolicy` shortcut: if the current frame has
  positive evidence and the mode's own `p_valid` is at least `0.88`, choose
  `TRUST` instead of another `VERIFY`.
- Passed current positive evidence from the Habitat RGB-noise runner into the
  shared decision context.

Reason:
- The first Grounding-DINO `geodesic_path` smoke showed that `memory=on`
  reduced gate rejections compared with `naive_count` but missed some
  target-visible success rows because it waited one extra verification frame
  after strong current positive evidence.

Verification:
- Wrote a failing policy test showing that the same high-validity belief stays
  `VERIFY` without current positive evidence but becomes `TRUST` with current
  positive evidence.
- Confirmed the test failed before implementation.
- Implemented the shared policy rule and reran focused local tests:
  `49` tests passed.
- Ran compile and whitespace checks.

Effect:
- This should reduce over-conservatism for `memory=on` in long-range replay
  without relaxing the current-positive gate or adding non-confirmation logic
  to `naive_count`.
- The rule is shared by all memory modes, so it is not a baseline-only or
  algorithm-only post-hoc metric change.

Follow-up:
- Pull on `badger-linux` and rerun the Grounding-DINO `geodesic_path` smoke to
  check success/gate-rejection tradeoff.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Delayed Birth To RGB-Noise Memory Replay

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added explicit candidate-birth state for `memory=on` in the RGB-noise
  replay harness.
- Fresh default memory now ignores pre-birth non-positive evidence until the
  first `POSITIVE` detector observation creates a candidate.
- Beliefs loaded from SQLite that differ from the default are treated as
  already born, preserving lifelong carry-over across episodes.
- `naive_count` remains unchanged and still only counts positive observations.

Reason:
- The first `geodesic_path` Grounding-DINO smoke exposed a fairness bug in the
  long-range setup: approach frames before the first visible/detected target
  decayed `memory=on`, while `naive_count` was unaffected because it ignores
  every non-positive event. That is not the intended delayed-birth algorithm
  boundary.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for pre-birth non-positive evidence and post-birth
  negative evidence behavior; the focused suite failed with the expected
  missing helper.
- Implemented the helper and reran
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py -q`;
  `38` tests passed.
- Ran focused local tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_usability_memory.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_lifelong_memory_harness.py -q`;
  `49` tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran geodesic-path preflight for `1280x720`, Grounding-DINO, and
  `on,naive_count,off`; summary wrote
  `runs/habitat_usability/geodesic_delayed_birth_preflight/summary.json`.

Effect:
- Long-range replay no longer penalizes `memory=on` before a candidate object
  has been born.
- Once born, memory still decays under non-confirmation/free/access-blocked or
  scene-change evidence, so stale-memory handling remains an algorithm
  contribution.

Follow-up:
- Pull this commit on `badger-linux` and rerun the same Grounding-DINO
  `geodesic_path` smoke to compare `memory=on` against `naive_count`.

  `naive_count`, or gate behavior.

  memory is more conservative, especially for `plant`.

Follow-up:
- Implement an expected-location-empty or stale-memory challenge before
  scaling to a larger paper-facing matrix.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Expected-Empty Replay Challenge

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added `replay_protocol=expected_empty_challenge`.
- The protocol emits `confirm`, `expected_empty`, and `revisit` phases from
  measured target-visible and target-hidden viewpoints.
- Added `ReplayStep.expected_target_absent` and a trace row field of the same
  name.
- During expected-empty frames, target-absent frames without detector positive
  evidence are converted to `NON_CONFIRMATION` with reason
  `expected_location_empty`.
- Detector positives in expected-empty frames remain positive, so false
  positives are still visible in the trace.

Reason:
- The geodesic smoke showed that repeated-positive long-range replay does not
  expose stale-memory or expected-empty behavior. The memory algorithm's
  contribution includes using negative evidence after a remembered-location
  verification; the harness needed an explicit way to create that evidence in
  Habitat without giving `naive_count` any negative-evidence logic.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for preflight protocol registration, replay step
  expected-empty marking, conversion to `NON_CONFIRMATION`, and preserving
  detector positives.
- Confirmed the focused suite failed before implementation:
  `4 failed, 39 passed`.
- Implemented the protocol and context helper, then reran
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py -q`;
  `43` tests passed.
- Ran broader focused local verification:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_usability_memory.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_lifelong_memory_harness.py -q`;
  `54` tests passed.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran expected-empty preflight and `git diff --check`.
- Pulled commit `c34e87f` on `badger-linux`; remote focused tests passed in
  `conda habitat`: `54` tests.
- Ran `oracle_bbox` expected-empty smoke:
  `runs/habitat_usability/expected_empty_oracle_bbox_smoke`.
  Expected-empty phase produced `36/36` `NON_CONFIRMATION` rows.
- Ran Grounding-DINO expected-empty smoke:
  `runs/habitat_usability/expected_empty_grounding_dino_smoke_1280x720_cap384`.
  Expected-empty phase produced `24` `NON_CONFIRMATION` rows and `12`
  detector-positive rows.

Effect:
- The next Linux smoke can test whether expected-empty frames produce true
  `NON_CONFIRMATION` and whether `memory=on` reduces stale trust relative to
  positive-only `naive_count`.
- The Linux smokes show the intended stale-trust separation:
  Grounding-DINO `memory=on` has `15` raw trust rows and `4` gate rejections
  versus `naive_count` with `27` raw trust rows and `12` gate rejections.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Memory Anchor FOV Consistency Check

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-objectnav-memory-validation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Extended the optional `memory=on` geometry gate with a field-of-view
  consistency check.
- If a remembered anchor is outside the current camera FOV but the detector
  reports the same category in the current frame, memory quarantines that
  positive as `UNKNOWN` with reason
  `geometry_anchor_out_of_view_positive`.
- `naive_count` remains unchanged and receives no geometry/FOV logic.

Reason:
- The first bed geometry-gate smoke showed that radius-only gating was
  partially useful but insufficient. It rejected episode `33` expected-empty
  false positives, but missed episode `3` red-dresser false positives because
  the coarse bbox-center depth projection landed within `0.86 m` of the bed
  anchor.
- In expected-empty turned-around views, the remembered bed anchor is behind
  the camera; a front-facing detector positive should not confirm that anchor
  even if its coarse projected depth is nearby.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for anchor-FOV checking and out-of-view positive
  quarantine.
- Confirmed the focused suite failed before implementation:
  `2 failed, 47 passed`.
- Implemented the FOV check and reran focused tests:
  `49` RGB-noise stress tests passed.
- Ran broader focused local verification:
  `60` tests passed across RGB-noise stress, usability memory, and lifelong
  harness tests.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran geometry-gate CLI preflight and `git diff --check`.

Effect:
- The next Linux bed smoke can test a more semantically meaningful
  memory-only association check: current detector positives are accepted only
  if they can plausibly observe the remembered anchor.

Follow-up:
- Pull this commit on `badger-linux` and rerun the bed expected-empty
  geometry-gate smoke.
- Compare geometry gate reasons, expected-empty positive/unknown counts,
  `memory=on` raw trust, gate rejections, and revisit success rows against the
  radius-only run.

## 2026-05-28 - Split Memory Geometry FOV And Distance Gates

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

Change:
- Split the optional memory geometry prototype into two controls:
  `--memory-geometry-gate-radius-m` for distance gating and
  `--memory-geometry-gate-fov` / `--no-memory-geometry-gate-fov` for camera
  FOV consistency.
- `memory_geometry_gate_radius_m <= 0` now disables only the distance gate.
- FOV gating can run by itself and is recorded in preflight summaries as
  `memory_geometry_gate_fov`.

Reason:
- The radius-only smoke rejected all bed expected-empty positives in episode
  `33`, but it also rejected three true revisit positives. FOV consistency is
  closer to the expected-location verification semantics and should be tested
  independently before enabling brittle distance thresholds in larger runs.

Verification:
- Wrote a failing test showing FOV gating should still run when radius gating
  is disabled.
- Confirmed the focused suite failed before implementation:
  `1 failed, 49 passed`.
- Implemented the split controls and reran focused tests:
  `50` RGB-noise stress tests passed.
- Ran broader focused local verification:
  `61` tests passed across RGB-noise stress, usability memory, and lifelong
  harness tests.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.
- Ran FOV-only CLI preflight and `git diff --check`.

Effect:
- The next Linux smoke can run FOV-only memory association with
  `--memory-geometry-gate-radius-m 0 --memory-geometry-gate-fov`, avoiding the
  observed radius-threshold revisit false negatives.

Follow-up:
- Pull on `badger-linux` and rerun the bed expected-empty smoke in FOV-only
  mode.

File:

- Added `replay_protocol=geodesic_expected_empty_challenge`.
- The protocol replays bounded Habitat navmesh shortest-path waypoints as
  `approach`, confirms the goal view, jumps to a target-hidden expected-empty
  view, then revisits the goal view.
- The expected-empty segment sets `ReplayStep.expected_target_absent=True`,
  reusing the existing expected-empty evidence conversion and memory geometry
  gate.

Reason:
- The short expected-empty replay isolates stale-memory handling, while the
  geodesic replay isolates official start-to-goal distance. The next
  paper-relevant replay needs both: long-range ObjectNav context plus a
  remembered-location-empty verification interval.

  construction.
- Confirmed the focused test subset failed before implementation:
  `2 failed`.
- Implemented the protocol and reran the new subset:
  `2 passed`.
- Ran the full RGB-noise stress focused file:
  `52` tests passed.
- Ran broader focused local verification:
  `63` tests passed across RGB-noise stress, usability memory, and lifelong
  harness tests.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.

Effect:
- The Linux Habitat runner can now test long-distance, replay-style lifelong
  memory behavior with Grounding-DINO, RGB/depth noise, `memory=on`,
  positive-only `naive_count`, and `memory=off` under the same shared gate.

Follow-up:
- Pull on `badger-linux`, run the same focused tests in `conda habitat`, then
  run a small `geodesic_expected_empty_challenge` matrix before scaling.

## 2026-05-28 - Scope Memory Geometry FOV Gate To Verification Phases

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

Change:
- Scoped the memory geometry FOV gate to `expected_empty` and `revisit`
  phases instead of applying it to every positive row.
- During `approach` and `confirm`, accepted positives can refresh the
  per-replay anchor while the object candidate is still being acquired.
- During expected-empty verification, an out-of-FOV same-category positive is
  converted to `NON_CONFIRMATION` for the remembered anchor rather than a
  neutral quarantined unknown.

Reason:
- The first `geodesic_expected_empty_challenge` smoke showed that early
  approach positives can create a poor anchor. Applying the FOV gate
  immediately caused later true goal-viewpoint positives to be rejected,
  especially for `bed` and `toilet`.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests for deferring FOV gating during anchor acquisition and
  converting expected-empty out-of-FOV positives into non-confirmation.
